It's as clear as if she is standing right there in the room with me.\n\nDoctor: Do you hear the woman's voice all the time or just sometimes?\n\nPatient: Well, it's not every single minute of the day, but it's pretty constant. There was... I guess there was a quieter spell for a few weeks a little while back. I was sleeping a lot more then, just feeling really tired and not rushing around cleaning as much as usual. But even then, I still heard her by the kitchen window. And I still knew the agency people were keeping an eye on us. So, the voice is pretty much always around in some way.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the hospital people and the agency are really watching your house and monitoring your care of your husband?\n\nPatient: Yes, absolutely. I know other people act like I'm just imagining it all, but I'm not. I know for a fact that the hospital and the agency folks are watching our house. They even track my cleaning routes when I'm working. They want to see if I'm taking proper care of my husband. So, yes, I believe it, because I know it's really happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to keep your thoughts straight or organized when you're talking about what's been going on?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. It's... well, when I try to explain what is happening, my mind just kind of goes all over the place. I'll start talking about my husband's heart pills, and then suddenly I'm talking about the church schedule, and then... then it's the numbers on my client list for work. It makes sense to me when I'm saying it, but people tell me they can't follow me. They say I jump around too much.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty completing tasks because your thoughts are disorganized?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my thoughts get a little jumbled when I'm trying to talk to people, like I said, but it doesn't stop me from getting my work done. I still clean the houses on my route, and I still take care of my husband. The tasks themselves are fine, I just... I just have a hard time explaining things sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble behaving appropriately in social situations?\n\nPatient: Well, I'm not sure about social situations exactly, but I do catch myself doing some odd things. Like, at work or even at home, I'll start lining up the bottles, the towels, and the chairs in these certain patterns. And then I end up walking back through the rooms over and over again. It just... it feels wrong to leave them any other way. I suppose if someone is watching me do that, it probably looks a bit strange to them.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel compelled to perform these lining up rituals or checking behaviors in response to the voice you hear or because of the people watching you?\n\nPatient: No, it's not because of a voice. And I don't do it because of people watching me, either. I just... I don't know, I just do it because it feels wrong if I don't. It's just this feeling I get that the bottles and things need to be lined up a certain way. Nobody is telling me to do it, it's just something I have to do so the room feels right.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. So, the voice is pretty much always around in some way.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the hospital people and the agency are really watching your house and monitoring your care of your husband?\n\nPatient: Yes, absolutely. I know other people act like I'm just imagining it all, but I'm not. I know for a fact that the hospital and the agency folks are watching our house. They even track my cleaning routes when I'm working. They want to see if I'm taking proper care of my husband. So, yes, I believe it, because I know it's really happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to keep your thoughts straight or organized when you're talking about what's been going on?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. It's... well, when I try to explain what is happening, my mind just kind of goes all over the place. I'll start talking about my husband's heart pills, and then suddenly I'm talking about the church schedule, and then... then it's the numbers on my client list for work. It makes sense to me when I'm saying it, but people tell me they can't follow me. They say I jump around too much.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty completing tasks because your thoughts are disorganized?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I just have a hard time explaining things sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble behaving appropriately in social situations?\n\nPatient: Well, I'm not sure about social situations exactly, but I do catch myself doing some odd things. Like, at work or even at home, I'll start lining up the bottles, the towels, and the chairs in these certain patterns. And then I end up walking back through the rooms over and over again. It just... it feels wrong to leave them any other way. I suppose if someone is watching me do that, it probably looks a bit strange to them.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel compelled to perform these lining up rituals or checking behaviors in response to the voice you hear or because of the people watching you?\n\nPatient: No, it's not because of a voice. It's just this feeling I get that the bottles and things need to be lined up a certain way. Nobody is telling me to do it, it's just something I have to do so the room feels right.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned the hospital people and agency are watching you, can you tell me more about what you think their goal is in watching your house and your care of your husband?\n\nPatient: Well... honestly, I'm really only here because people keep acting like I'm imagining the whole thing. Um, even when the house is completely quiet, I hear a woman's voice. She sounds like she's right near the kitchen window. She tells me to check the locks, or... or to move his pills. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I'm here because... people keep acting like I'm just imagining things. But I know what I see. The hospital people, and some folks from the agency... they've been watching our house. They even watch my cleaning routes when I'm at work. They're trying to see if I'm taking proper care of my husband. So, I guess I'm here because no one wants to listen to me about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or sounds that other people can't hear?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. Um, even when the house is completely quiet, I hear a woman's voice. She sounds like she's right near the kitchen window. She tells me to check the locks, or... or to move his pills. And it's not just a whisper or something in my head. It's as clear as if she is standing right there in the room with me.\n\nDoctor: Do you hear the woman's voice all the time or just sometimes?\n\nPatient: Well, it's not every single minute of the day, but it's pretty constant. There was... I guess there was a quieter spell for a few weeks a little while back. I was sleeping a lot more then, just feeling really tired and not rushing around cleaning as much as usual. But even then, I still heard her by the kitchen window. And I still knew the agency people were keeping an eye on us. So, the voice is pretty much always around in some way.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the hospital people and the agency are really watching your house and monitoring your care of your husband?\n\nPatient: Yes, absolutely. I know other people act like I'm just imagining it all, but I'm not. I know for a fact that the hospital and the agency folks are watching our house. They even track my cleaning routes when I'm working. They want to see if I'm taking proper care of my husband. So, yes, I believe it, because I know it's really happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to keep your thoughts straight or organized when you're talking about what's been going on?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. It's... well, when I try to explain what is happening, my mind just kind of goes all over the place. I'll start talking about my husband's heart pills, and then suddenly I'm talking about the church schedule, and then... then it's the numbers on my client list for work. It makes sense to me when I'm saying it, but people tell me they can't follow me. They say I jump around too much.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty completing tasks because your thoughts are disorganized?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my thoughts get a little jumbled when I'm trying to talk to people, like I said, but it doesn't stop me from getting my work done. I still clean the houses on my route, and I still take care of my husband. The tasks themselves are fine, I just... I just have a hard time explaining things sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble behaving appropriately in social situations?\n\nPatient: Well, I'm not sure about social situations exactly, but I do catch myself doing some odd things. Like, at work or even at home, I'll start lining up the bottles, the towels, and the chairs in these certain patterns. And then I end up walking back through the rooms over and over again. It just... it feels wrong to leave them any other way. I suppose if someone is watching me do that, it probably looks a bit strange to them.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel compelled to perform these lining up rituals or checking behaviors in response to the voice you hear or because of the people watching you?\n\nPatient: No, it's not because of a voice. And I don't do it because of people watching me, either. I just... I don't know, I just do it because it feels wrong if I don't. It's just this feeling I get that the bottles and things need to be lined up a certain way. But no, I don't have any problem following what other people are saying.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to make decisions or complete tasks on your own because your thoughts are disorganized?\n\nPatient: Well, it's not so much that I can't decide what to do... it's more about how I have to do it. And I'll end up walking back through the rooms over and over again to check them. It just... it feels wrong to leave them any other way, and I get stuck doing that instead of just finishing the job.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you're not able to line things up in your preferred patterns?\n\nPatient: No, not really anxious, I guess. But even then, I still heard her by the kitchen window. And I still knew the agency people were keeping an eye on us. So, the voice is pretty much always around in some way.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the hospital people and the agency are really watching your house and monitoring your care of your husband?\n\nPatient: Yes, absolutely. I know other people act like I'm just imagining it all, but I'm not. I know for a fact that the hospital and the agency folks are watching our house. They even track my cleaning routes when I'm working. They want to see if I'm taking proper care of my husband. So, yes, I believe it, because I know it's really happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to keep your thoughts straight or organized when you're talking about what's been going on?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. It's... well, when I try to explain what is happening, my mind just kind of goes all over the place. I'll start talking about my husband's heart pills, and then suddenly I'm talking about the church schedule, and then... then it's the numbers on my client list for work. It makes sense to me when I'm saying it, but people tell me they can't follow me. They say I jump around too much.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty completing tasks because your thoughts are disorganized?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my thoughts get a little jumbled when I'm trying to talk to people, like I said, but it doesn't stop me from getting my work done. I still clean the houses on my route, and I still take care of my husband. The tasks themselves are fine, I just... I just have a hard time explaining things sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble behaving appropriately in social situations?\n\nPatient: Well, I'm not sure about social situations exactly, but I do catch myself doing some odd things. It just... it feels wrong to leave them any other way. I suppose if someone is watching me do that, it probably looks a bit strange to them.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel compelled to perform these lining up rituals or checking behaviors in response to the voice you hear or because of the people watching you?\n\nPatient: No, it's not because of a voice. It's just this feeling I get that the bottles and things need to be lined up a certain way. Nobody is telling me to do it, it's just something I have to do so the room feels right.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned the hospital people and agency are watching you, can you tell me more about what you think their goal is in watching your house and your care of your husband?\n\nPatient: Well... honestly, I'm really only here because people keep acting like I'm imagining the whole thing. It's... well, like I said before, it's more that they have trouble understanding me when my words run together. But no, I don't have any problem following what other people are saying.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to make decisions or complete tasks on your own because your thoughts are disorganized?\n\nPatient: Well, it's not so much that I can't decide what to do... it's more about how I have to do it. And I'll end up walking back through the rooms over and over again to check them. It just... it feels wrong to leave them any other way, and I get stuck doing that instead of just finishing the job.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you're not able to line things up in your preferred patterns?\n\nPatient: No, not really anxious, I guess. Um, even when the house is completely quiet, I hear a woman's voice. She sounds like she's right near the kitchen window. She tells me to check the locks, or... or to move his pills. And it's not just a whisper or something in my head. There was... I guess there was a quieter spell for a few weeks a little while back. I was sleeping a lot more then, just feeling really tired and not rushing around cleaning as much as usual. But even then, I still heard her by the kitchen window. And I still knew the agency people were keeping an eye on us. So, the voice is pretty much always around in some way.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the hospital people and the agency are really watching your house and monitoring your care of your husband?\n\nPatient: Yes, absolutely. I know other people act like I'm just imagining it all, but I'm not. I know for a fact that the hospital and the agency folks are watching our house. They even track my cleaning routes when I'm working. They want to see if I'm taking proper care of my husband. So, yes, I believe it, because I know it's really happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to keep your thoughts straight or organized when you're talking about what's been going on?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. It's... well, when I try to explain what is happening, my mind just kind of goes all over the place. I'll start talking about my husband's heart pills, and then suddenly I'm talking about the church schedule, and then... then it's the numbers on my client list for work. It makes sense to me when I'm saying it, but people tell me they can't follow me. They say I jump around too much.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty completing tasks because your thoughts are disorganized?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my thoughts get a little jumbled when I'm trying to talk to people, like I said, but it doesn't stop me from getting my work done. I still clean the houses on my route, and I still take care of my husband. The tasks themselves are fine, I just... I just have a hard time explaining things sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble behaving appropriately in social situations?\n\nPatient: Well, I'm not sure about social situations exactly, but I do catch myself doing some odd things. Like, at work or even at home, I'll start lining up the bottles, the towels, and the chairs in these certain patterns. And then I end up walking back through the rooms over and over again. It just... it feels wrong to leave them any other way. I suppose if someone is watching me do that, it probably looks a bit strange to them.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel compelled to perform these lining up rituals or checking behaviors in response to the voice you hear or because of the people watching you?\n\nPatient: No, it's not because of a voice. Nobody is telling me to do it, it's just something I have to do so the room feels right.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned the hospital people and agency are watching you, can you tell me more about what you think their goal is in watching your house and your care of your husband?\n\nPatient: Well... honestly, I'm really only here because people keep acting like I'm imagining the whole thing. They're doing it to see if I'm taking proper care of my husband. It's... well, like I said before, it's more that they have trouble understanding me when my words run together. It just... it feels wrong to leave them any other way, and I get stuck doing that instead of just finishing the job.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you're not able to line things up in your preferred patterns?\n\nPatient: No, not really anxious, I guess. I mean, it's not like I get scared or panicky. It just... bothers me. It feels like the job isn't done right if they aren't lined up. I just prefer it a certain way, so I fix it. Um, even when the house is completely quiet, I hear a woman's voice. She sounds like she's right near the kitchen window. She tells me to check the locks, or... or to move his pills. But even then, I still heard her by the kitchen window. And I still knew the agency people were keeping an eye on us. I know other people act like I'm just imagining it all, but I'm not. I know for a fact that the hospital and the agency folks are watching our house. They even track my cleaning routes when I'm working. They want to see if I'm taking proper care of my husband. So, yes, I believe it, because I know it's really happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to keep your thoughts straight or organized when you're talking about what's been going on?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. It's... well, when I try to explain what is happening, my mind just kind of goes all over the place. I'll start talking about my husband's heart pills, and then suddenly I'm talking about the church schedule, and then... then it's the numbers on my client list for work. It makes sense to me when I'm saying it, but people tell me they can't follow me. They say I jump around too much.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty completing tasks because your thoughts are disorganized?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my thoughts get a little jumbled when I'm trying to talk to people, like I said, but it doesn't stop me from getting my work done. I still clean the houses on my route, and I still take care of my husband. The tasks themselves are fine, I just... I just have a hard time explaining things sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble behaving appropriately in social situations?\n\nPatient: Well, I'm not sure about social situations exactly, but I do catch myself doing some odd things. It just... it feels wrong to leave them any other way. I suppose if someone is watching me do that, it probably looks a bit strange to them.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel compelled to perform these lining up rituals or checking behaviors in response to the voice you hear or because of the people watching you?\n\nPatient: No, it's not because of a voice. But no, I don't have any problem following what other people are saying.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to make decisions or complete tasks on your own because your thoughts are disorganized?\n\nPatient: Well, it's not so much that I can't decide what to do... it's more about how I have to do it. They're trying to see if I'm taking proper care of my husband. Um, even when the house is completely quiet, I hear a woman's voice. She sounds like she's right near the kitchen window. She tells me to check the locks, or... or to move his pills. And it's not just a whisper or something in my head. It's as clear as if she is standing right there in the room with me.\n\nDoctor: Do you hear the woman's voice all the time or just sometimes?\n\nPatient: Well, it's not every single minute of the day, but it's pretty constant. There was... I guess there was a quieter spell for a few weeks a little while back. I was sleeping a lot more then, just feeling really tired and not rushing around cleaning as much as usual. But even then, I still heard her by the kitchen window. And I still knew the agency people were keeping an eye on us. So, the voice is pretty much always around in some way.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the hospital people and the agency are really watching your house and monitoring your care of your husband?\n\nPatient: Yes, absolutely. I know other people act like I'm just imagining it all, but I'm not. I know for a fact that the hospital and the agency folks are watching our house. They even track my cleaning routes when I'm working. They want to see if I'm taking proper care of my husband. So, yes, I believe it, because I know it's really happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to keep your thoughts straight or organized when you're talking about what's been going on?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. It's... well, when I try to explain what is happening, my mind just kind of goes all over the place. I'll start talking about my husband's heart pills, and then suddenly I'm talking about the church schedule, and then... then it's the numbers on my client list for work. It makes sense to me when I'm saying it, but people tell me they can't follow me. They say I jump around too much.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty completing tasks because your thoughts are disorganized?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my thoughts get a little jumbled when I'm trying to talk to people, like I said, but it doesn't stop me from getting my work done. I still clean the houses on my route, and I still take care of my husband. The tasks themselves are fine, I just... I just have a hard time explaining things sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble behaving appropriately in social situations?\n\nPatient: Well, I'm not sure about social situations exactly, but I do catch myself doing some odd things. Like, at work or even at home, I'll start lining up the bottles, the towels, and the chairs in these certain patterns. And then I end up walking back through the rooms over and over again. It just... it feels wrong to leave them any other way. I suppose if someone is watching me do that, it probably looks a bit strange to them.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel compelled to perform these lining up rituals or checking behaviors in response to the voice you hear or because of the people watching you?\n\nPatient: No, it's not because of a voice. And I don't do it because of people watching me, either. I just... I don't know, I just do it because it feels wrong if I don't. It's just this feeling I get that the bottles and things need to be lined up a certain way. Nobody is telling me to do it, it's just something I have to do so the room feels right.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned the hospital people and agency are watching you, can you tell me more about what you think their goal is in watching your house and your care of your husband?\n\nPatient: Well... honestly, I'm really only here because people keep acting like I'm imagining the whole thing. But I'm not. I know for a fact that the hospital people and some folks from the agency have been watching our house. They even watch me out on my cleaning routes. They're doing it to see if I'm taking proper care of my husband. That's what they're looking for, just checking up on how I take care of him.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it difficult to stay on one topic when talking to others?\n\nPatient: I guess I do, yes. When I try to explain what is happening with everything, my words just sort of... run together. I'll start talking about my husband's heart pills, and then suddenly I'm on about the church schedule, or the numbers on my client list for my cleaning jobs. People tell me they can't follow me when I talk like that, because I just jump from one thing to the next.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling emotionally numb or detached from things that would normally affect you?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I still feel things normally, I guess. I mean, I get upset or happy just like anyone else. I haven't really noticed feeling numb or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble understanding other people when they talk to you?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I understand folks just fine. If one of my cleaning clients tells me to do something, or if my husband asks for something, I know exactly what they're saying. It's... well, like I said before, it's more that they have trouble understanding me when my words run together. But no, I don't have any problem following what other people are saying.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to make decisions or complete tasks on your own because your thoughts are disorganized?\n\nPatient: Well, it's not so much that I can't decide what to do... it's more about how I have to do it. Like, at work or even at home, I'll catch myself lining up the cleaning bottles, or folding the towels, or pushing chairs in, but they have to be in certain patterns. And I'll end up walking back through the rooms over and over again to check them. It just... it feels wrong to leave them any other way, and I get stuck doing that instead of just finishing the job.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you're not able to line things up in your preferred patterns?\n\nPatient: No, not really anxious, I guess. I mean, it's not like I get scared or panicky. It just... bothers me. It feels like the job isn't done right if they aren't lined up. I just prefer it a certain way, so I fix it. But I wouldn't call it anxiety.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people watching you are trying to harm you or your husband?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think they want to hurt us. I'm here because people keep acting like I'm imagining it, but I know the hospital people and some folks from the agency have been watching our house and my cleaning routes to see if I'm taking proper care of my husband. They aren't trying to harm us, they're just... checking up on me, I guess. Watching to see if I mess up.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the hospital people and agency are watching you because of something specific you've done?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think it's because of anything I did. I mean, I haven't done anything wrong. I just go to work, do my cleaning, and come home to my husband. I really don't know why they picked me to keep tabs on... I just know that they are doing it.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned the hospital people and agency are watching you, can you describe exactly what you think they hope to achieve or learn by watching your house and your care of your husband?\n\nPatient: I honestly don't know. Like I said before, it just feels like they want to see if I make a mistake taking care of my husband, or maybe with my cleaning jobs. But as for exactly what they want to achieve by doing all this? I couldn't tell you. I just know they're out there keeping tabs on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voice you hear or the people watching you are telling you to line things up in certain patterns?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think it's the people watching me or any voices telling me to do that. I mean, I haven't done anything wrong. I just go to work, do my cleaning, and come home to my husband. I really don't know why they picked me to keep tabs on... I just know that they are doing it.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned the hospital people and agency are watching you, can you describe exactly what you think they hope to achieve or learn by watching your house and your care of your husband?\n\nPatient: I honestly don't know. I mean... I don't know what their ultimate goal is or what they hope to get out of it. Like I said before, it just feels like they want to see if I make a mistake taking care of my husband, or maybe with my cleaning jobs. But as for exactly what they want to achieve by doing all this? I couldn't tell you. I just know they're out there keeping tabs on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voice you hear or the people watching you are telling you to line things up in certain patterns?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think it's the people watching me or any voices telling me to do that. I just do it. Whether I'm at work cleaning or at home, I catch myself lining up the bottles, towels, and chairs in these certain patterns. And then I end up walking back through the rooms over and over again. Nobody is making me do it, it just... it feels wrong to leave them any other way.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voice or your belief in being watched is causing you to line things up in certain patterns?\n\nPatient: No, like I was just saying, I really don't think they are causing it. I just catch myself doing it on my own. Whether I'm at work or at home, I end up lining up the bottles, the towels, and the chairs in these certain patterns. And then I walk back through the rooms over and over again. It just feels wrong to leave things any other way. It's not because the voice or the people watching are making me do it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that the hospital people and agency might not actually be watching you?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think there's any possibility of that. I know it sounds crazy to other people, and I'm here because people keep acting like I'm imagining it. But I'm not. I know for a fact that the hospital people and some folks from the agency have been watching our house. They watch my cleaning routes, too. They want to see if I'm taking proper care of my husband. I know what I see, and they are definitely watching me."
